# DripNepal: Risks and Open Decisions

Status: Draft v1 (2026-09-25)

Reviewed: 2026-09-26 · consistency review 2026-09-30 to 2026-10-04

This document is the single source of truth for three registers:

- **Open decisions (OD-01 … OD-65).** Questions a named owner must answer before a milestone can start or finish.
- **External verifications (VX-01 … VX-18).** Legal, tax, provider and pricing facts that an authoritative party must confirm before the design relies on them.
- **Risks (R-01 … R-33).** Things that could go wrong for DripNepal specifically, with scores, mitigations, owners and early warnings.

Other documents cite these IDs and do not restate the content. When a decision is made, the ruling moves into the owning document (and an ADR where it is structural). This register keeps the date, the decision text and the links. Evidence labels ([Confirmed], [Verified-repo], [Verified-doc], [Assumption], [Open], [Verify-external]) are defined in [00 §1.2](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#12-evidence-labels). Assumptions A-xx live in [00](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md), not here.

External facts below come from the [research notes](research/README.md) compiled on 2026-09-25. Each one carries its source URL, and every URL was accessed on 2026-09-25. Nothing in this register claims that DripNepal is compliant with any law; legal readings are [Verify-external] until the named verifier confirms them in writing. English texts of Nepali laws are translations and **the Nepali text prevails** (see R-23).

## 1. How to use this register

### 1.1 What belongs here and what does not

An item belongs here if it is still undecided, still unverified, or still a live risk. A settled rule belongs in the owning document. For example, once OD-06 is decided, the numbers go into `platform_settings` defaults in [04](04-domain-model-and-data-dictionary.md) and the completion rule in [05](05-order-payment-and-inventory-lifecycles.md). The OD row here changes to Decided and records the date, the ruling and the links.

Writers of other documents must not invent new OD, VX or R numbers. They add a request under "Consistency notes for editor" in their own document, and the tech lead assigns the next free number here during the weekly review.

### 1.2 Owners

DripNepal has 1–2 developers and a small budget [Confirmed] (Q1), so "owner" means a role, and one person may hold several roles.

| Role                    | Who, in practice                                               | Decides or verifies                                                                                                                                                        |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Product owner**       | The business owner of DripNepal, who also owns the repository  | Business policy ODs (commission, COD, returns, SLAs, moderation, brand policy, repo visibility). Approves every recommendation before it becomes Decided.                  |
| **Tech lead**           | The lead developer                                             | Technical ODs (OD-08, OD-09, OD-12, OD-13, OD-25). Runs the weekly review and maintains this file.                                                                         |
| **Legal counsel**       | An external Nepali lawyer engaged per question pack (§3.2)     | VX-01 … VX-04, VX-08, VX-09 and the legal side of OD-02, OD-07, OD-16, OD-17, OD-21, OD-24, OD-26, OD-35, OD-58. Must check the Nepali original of every clause relied on. |
| **Accountant**          | An external chartered accountant or tax adviser                | VX-05, and OD-11, OD-26, OD-27, OD-36, OD-58 (COD records). Signs off statement and payout formats.                                                                        |
| **Provider onboarding** | Whoever holds merchant relationships (the product owner in R1) | Written confirmations from eSewa, Khalti, Nepal Can Move, the email provider and hosting vendors (VX-06, VX-07, VX-14 to VX-18).                                           |

### 1.3 Status values

| Register | Status       | Meaning                                                                                                                                                 |
| -------- | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| OD       | **Open**     | No recommendation yet, or the recommendation depends on an unresolved VX.                                                                               |
| OD       | **Proposed** | A recommendation is written below. The owning docs already use the recommended default, labelled [Assumption] or [Open]. The owner has not approved it. |
| OD       | **Decided**  | The owner approved a ruling. The date, ruling and ADR link are recorded in §2.3. The owning documents may still need updating.                          |
| OD       | **Resolved** | Decided, and every referencing document and any code have been updated. No [Open] label for this OD remains in the docs set.                            |
| VX       | **Open**     | No primary source yet, or the primary sources are silent or contradict each other on the point that matters.                                            |
| VX       | **Proposed** | Primary evidence has been gathered (listed in §3.1). The named verifier has not confirmed it in writing.                                                |
| VX       | **Resolved** | The verifier confirmed it in writing. The date and the reference to the written opinion or provider email are recorded.                                 |
| Risk     | **Open**     | Live risk. Mitigations are planned or in place.                                                                                                         |
| Risk     | **Resolved** | The cause is gone (for example, VX-01 answered favourably), or the owner formally accepted the residual risk.                                           |

### 1.4 Review cadence

- **Weekly during R0 and R1** (M0–M7): 30 minutes, product owner and tech lead. Fixed agenda:
  1. New evidence on any VX, and replies from counsel, the accountant or providers.
  2. Every OD whose latest responsible moment falls within the next two weeks, per the milestone plan in [12](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md). Decide it, or record the fallback in the table.
  3. Every risk scoring 12 or more, plus any risk whose trigger fired this week.
  4. Commit the changes to this file in the same session.
- **Fortnightly during R1.1** (M8), **monthly from R2**.
- **Event-driven reviews**, held within 3 working days of the event:
  - a new Finance Act (effective from Shrawan 1, the start of Nepal's fiscal year; see VX-05);
  - an NRB circular on payment aggregation;
  - a DoCSCP or IRD notice;
  - a change to eSewa or Khalti documentation;
  - a security incident;
  - a milestone slipping by more than two weeks.

Legal counsel and the accountant are not asked to attend. They receive a question pack (§3.2) and answer in writing, which keeps a small budget to one or two paid engagements before launch.

### 1.5 Recording a decision

When an OD becomes Decided:

1. Change the status in §2.2. Add an entry to §2.3 with the date (AD, Asia/Kathmandu), the ruling in one or two sentences, who decided, and links.
2. If the ruling is structural or expensive to reverse, write or supersede an ADR in [adr/](adr/README.md) and link it. Examples: collection model, hosting, invoicing, UI kit tier, framework upgrade. Settings values (COD limits, SLA hours) need no ADR; the §2.3 entry is enough.
3. Update the owning documents named in the [00 §1.4 ownership map](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#14-document-ownership-map), following [00 §1.5](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#15-how-a-decision-changes). Remove the `[Open] OD-xx` labels. Update traceability in [12](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md) if scope moved.
4. Commit with a message such as `docs: decide OD-18 COD limits`. The OD becomes Resolved when step 3 is complete.

The decision log entry has this format:

```text
OD-xx — Decided 2026-MM-DD by <role>
Ruling: <one or two sentences>
Evidence: <VX-xx resolved / opinion reference / data used>
ADR: <ADR-00NN link or "none (setting value)">
Docs updated: <list>  |  Status: Decided → Resolved on <date>
```

### 1.6 Latest responsible moment and safe defaults

The **latest responsible moment (LRM)** is the last point at which the decision can be made without rework or a slipped release. Milestone dates live in [12](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md), so LRMs here are stated relative to milestone events, for example "before the first M5 placeOrder PR merges".

Many ODs set **values stored in `platform_settings`** (COD limits, SLA hours, hold days). Code for these is not blocked: developers build against the default in [04a §15.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#151-platform_settings) and the value changes later without a deploy. For those ODs the LRM is the launch gate, not the milestone start. Section 5 separates decisions that block **code** from decisions that block **launch**.

### 1.7 Risk scoring

Score = likelihood (L) × impact (I), each rated 1–5 on these DripNepal-specific scales:

| Rating | Likelihood (within the next 12 months) | Impact                                                                                                                    |
| ------ | -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1      | Very unlikely                          | Cosmetic. No customer or vendor notices.                                                                                  |
| 2      | Unlikely                               | Rework under 1 week, or a minor cost (under about Rs 10,000 a month) [Assumption].                                        |
| 3      | Possible                               | A feature or milestone slips 1–4 weeks, or limited customer harm that is refundable and fixed within days.                |
| 4      | Likely                                 | Launch or R1.1 blocked, money lost or mis-posted, a regulator fine is possible, or an outage over 4 h (the proposed RTO). |
| 5      | Almost certain                         | Enforced shutdown, criminal exposure for staff, customer money held unlawfully, or a mass personal-data breach.           |

Bands: **15–25 High** (reviewed weekly until below 15), **8–14 Medium**, **1–7 Low**.

## 2. Open decisions (OD-01 … OD-54)

### 2.1 How the decisions depend on each other

Most ODs are independent settings. A few legal and tax decisions chain together and gate R1 launch or M8. The diagram shows only those chains. Solid arrows mean "must be answered first". The dotted arrow is the COD part of OD-27 (see §5).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    VX01["VX-01 NRB licensing"] --> OD02["OD-02 Collection model"]
    OD02 --> OD03["OD-03 First gateway"]
    VX06["VX-06 eSewa capabilities"] --> OD03
    VX07["VX-07 Khalti capabilities"] --> OD03
    OD03 --> M8["M8 Gateway payments"]
    VX05["VX-05 VAT, e-billing, TDS"] --> OD11["OD-11 Tax treatment"]
    VX05 --> OD26["OD-26 Invoicing model"]
    VX05 --> OD27["OD-27 Advance tax 1 percent"]
    VX09["VX-09 Hosting location"] --> OD09["OD-09 Hosting"]
    OD26 --> OD09
    OD11 --> AGR["Seller agreement text (M2)"]
    OD04["OD-04 Commission"] --> AGR
    OD05["OD-05 COD remittance"] --> AGR
    VX02["VX-02 E-Commerce Act"] --> OD16["OD-16 KYC documents"]
    OD16 --> AGR
    AGR --> LAUNCH["R1 launch gate (M7 exit)"]
    OD09 --> LAUNCH
    OD26 --> LAUNCH
    OD27 --> M8
    OD27 -.-> LAUNCH
    OD27 --> OD36["OD-36 Tax on a failed payout"]
    OD36 --> M8
```

Of the ODs registered from the deep audits (OD-28 … OD-54), a few depend on another decision. OD-36 needs OD-27's answer first (in the diagram). OD-35 is decided together with OD-07 and OD-05, because it is another way of paying COD refunds, and OD-46 together with OD-05 and OD-14. OD-32 takes the payer of a return lost on the way back from OD-30. The reveal part of OD-38 applies only if OD-49 approves the staff reveals. The others can be decided on their own. OD-58 (COD on platform-owned courier accounts) was re-decided for R1 on 2026-11-22 as option (b), which needs counsel's reading of REG-27 and VX-01, the accountant, and VX-17 and VX-18 before the first live COD shipment on such an account. OD-63 (the legal posture of the single-seller launch, 2026-11-23) needs counsel's reading of VX-02 and is answered with REG-01 and the DoCSCP listing of REG-03, before the launch goes live.

### 2.2 Decision table

"Blocks" gives the milestone whose start or exit cannot pass without the decision. Where one decision gates several milestones, each is listed with the part of the decision it needs. §5 lists the same gates by milestone, and [12 §2.5](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#25-how-decisions-gate-a-milestone) follows §5.

OD-28 to OD-54 register the owner decisions that the three deep audits left open: Audit 1 of 05 (A1-xxx, [05 Consistency note 20](05-order-payment-and-inventory-lifecycles.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)), Audit 4 of 07 (A4-xxx, [07 Consistency notes 4, 7, 18, 20–24](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)) and Audit 2 of 04 and 04a (A2-xxx, IN-xx, the [Deep audit 2 note of 04](04-domain-model-and-data-dictionary.md#deep-audit-2-2026-09-30)). Each row names its audit IDs, and the audit notes keep the detail. A row that asks to approve a departure the docs already use is Proposed, with the recommendation to approve. "Tech-lead lean" marks a first view on an Open row; it is not yet a recommendation the docs use.

| OD-06 | What are the ledger hold (`ledger_hold_days`, default 7) and the return window (`return_window_days`, default 7)?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Return window 7 days (CPA s14 dissatisfaction return). Optionally 15 days for sealed goods (CPA s14(5)); this needs a second settings key (for example `sealed_return_window_days`) and a product-level sealed flag in [04a §15.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#151-platform_settings), plus a matching precondition in 05 §6.6. Hold = return window, or return window + return transit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | **Keep 7/7 for R1.** Under COD the vendor already holds the cash; the hold only sets when an entry counts as available. **Before M8 payouts**, raise the hold to at least return window + 7 days, so that a late-arriving return can be netted before money leaves. Counsel must confirm whether CPA s14 applies to online sales through E-Commerce Act s31 and whether the 15-day sealed-goods window binds DripNepal (VX-04). [Assumption A-49, [05 §7.2](05-order-payment-and-inventory-lifecycles.md#72-entry-types-and-posting-rules)] Ledger availability group rule: each delivery posting is one group, decided by its net. A group that nets positive (the platform owes the vendor), its commission debit included, waits `ledger_hold_days`; a group that nets zero or negative is available at once, as is every other entry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Product owner + legal counsel                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | M6 exit (values: shop orders complete after the window); M8 exit (hold revision before the first payout).                     | Values before R1 launch; hold revision before the first M8 payout                                                                                               | Proposed                                                               |
| OD-07 | Returns and refunds: who pays return shipping, which categories are excluded (innerwear, swimwear), and how are COD customers refunded?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | (a) Vendor pays for non-conforming goods; customer pays for change of mind. (b) Platform subsidises returns. (c) Free returns for all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | **(a) for non-conforming goods** (`not_as_described`, `damaged`, `wrong_item`): full refund including tax (E-Commerce Act s10). **Change of mind** within the window: refund without deduction from the price (CPA s14(2)). Counsel decides whether the customer may bear return transport (VX-04); Directive s8(3) bars charges after handover beyond the pre-agreed price and transport. Hygiene items are returnable only with the seal unbroken (CPA s14(4)–(5)). COD refunds go by `manual_transfer` to a bank or wallet account the customer names, never store credit. [Assumption A-48, 05 §7.4] The platform pays the COD refund within the 7-day deadline and recovers it from the vendor through the ledger, so the OD-05 terms must cover that recovery (R-03). | OD-26 | Who issues tax invoices and receipts for each sale, and must DripNepal operate IRD e-invoicing?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | (a) Vendors invoice from their own billing; DripNepal issues a non-tax order receipt. (b) DripNepal integrates an IRD-listed e-billing provider. (c) DripNepal builds IRD-listed billing software. (d) The IRD-provided billing system (the Finance Act 2083 booklet says IRD may provide one).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | **The accountant and counsel decide.** Engineering position: (c) is out of reach for 1–2 developers. It needs IRD listing, a security test report, a server located in Nepal and a tripartite agreement (Electronic Invoice Procedure 2082 s7). Keep invoice tables out of the R1 schema except the order receipt and snapshots, behind an `InvoiceIssuer` adapter boundary so that (b) or (d) can be added. The key question: does Directive 2082 s9(2) (e-invoice mandatory for cash payments) mean COD sales need e-invoices, and issued by whom? Current scope: 01 FR-ORD-007 (customer invoice) stays R2, and R1 sends a receipt email at COD `collected` (05 §6.5); vendors issue tax bills (A-16). | OD-62 | Should verifying an email also prove the password was set by whoever holds the mailbox? A stranger can sign up with someone else's address and a password of their own: the account stays `pending_verification` under that address, and if the holder follows the "Confirm your email" link, `confirmEmail` activates it without asking for the password and without rotating `security_stamp`, so the stranger can sign in to an `active` account under the holder's address until the holder resets the password. A repeat `signUp` for a still-pending account never touches the row, and the "account already exists" email is limited per sending network (`signup_ip`) only, not per holder (found in the Fable review of M1 part 2a, 2026-11-23; [07 TM-09](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#tm-09-account-recovery-abuse-and-enumeration))                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | (a) A repeat `signUp` for an address whose account is still `pending_verification` replaces the password hash and sends a new verification link instead of the account-exists email (the same 202), and `confirmEmail` rotates `security_stamp`, so a session signed in before verification ends. (b) As (a), and `confirmEmail` also requires the account's password: the link opens a page that asks for it, so activation proves both the mailbox and the password. (c) Accept and document: the verification email's "ignore" line is the only defence, and the holder recovers with a password reset. Costs of (a) and (b): anyone who knows the address can keep replacing a pending account's password hash and link until the holder verifies, limited only by `signup_ip` per network; and (b)'s password check in `confirmEmail` needs its own limiter. With any option, consider a per-holder cap on the account-exists email (for example one a day). | No recommendation yet. Tech-lead lean: (b). (a) alone moves the window to whoever signed up last before verification, because the replaced hash is the stranger's if the stranger signs up after the holder; only (b) makes activation prove the password too, and its replacement step lets the real holder take a pending account back by signing up again. Its cost is a password prompt on the link, which also works on another device. **Safe default while open: (c)**, the sign-up of M1 part 2a as built ([06 §13.3](06-api-design.md#133-auth-and-self)); (a) or (b) changes `signUp`, `confirmEmail` and the `/verify-email` page of part 2b.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Tech lead (product owner approves)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | M1 exit (sign-up and verification, J-04)                                                                                      | Before M1 part 2b builds `/verify-email`; at the latest the M1 exit                                                                                             | Open                                                                   |
| OD-63 | Under which legal posture does the single-seller launch (R1-S, [01 §5.5](01-product-requirements.md#55-single-seller-launch-r1-s)) sell? The product owner decided on 2026-11-23 to launch as the only seller, through their own shop (§2.3). [01 §5.4](01-product-requirements.md#54-explicit-non-goals) lists "DripNepal-owned stock" as a non-goal and [01 REG-01](01-product-requirements.md#reg-01-operate-as-an-intermediary-not-a-list-based-seller) requires DripNepal to operate only as an intermediary, because selling its own stock would make it a "list-based" entity with the extra duties of E-Commerce Act s15 [Verify-external VX-02]. Sub-questions: does the owner sell as a legal entity separate from the one that operates the platform; which registration applies to each (firm registration, PAN or VAT, the DoCSCP listing as intermediary or list-based, REG-02, REG-03); which s15 duties apply; and which intermediary duties (seller agreement REG-04, seller documents REG-05, complaint register REG-10) apply when the platform and its only seller have the same owner. | (a) A separately registered entity is the seller: it holds the shop and accepts a seller agreement, and DripNepal stays an intermediary, so REG-01 and the 01 §5.4 non-goal hold and later vendors change nothing. (b) DripNepal's operating entity sells its own stock as a list-based entity under s15: REG-01 and the non-goal are withdrawn or changed, `platform_legal_disclosures.entity_type` says list-based, and the posture changes again, with a re-filing (ECD s3(3)), when other vendors join. (c) Another structure counsel proposes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | **No recommendation: a legal question for counsel (VX-02).** The build does not depend on the answer: the owner's shop is an ordinary `shops` row, and the disclosures page reads its entity type from `platform_legal_disclosures` ([04a §15.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#151-platform_settings)). (a) leaves the documents and the later move to many vendors unchanged; (b) changes 01 REG-01, §5.4 and the disclosures text now and again when vendors join. Tax registration and invoicing for the owner's sales are asked with it (OD-11, OD-26).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Product owner + legal counsel (accountant on registration and tax)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | R1-S go-live (launch part 7, [12 §9](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#9-single-seller-launch-r1-s)); not the build                   | Before the DoCSCP listing application for the launch (gate G3), which names the entity and says whether it is an intermediary or list-based                     | Open                                                                   |
| OD-64 | May the owner of an approved shop change its business type and business identifiers (registration number, registering authority, PAN/VAT number, VAT flag) directly with `updateShopProfile`, or does such a change need review? Staff review these details at application, with the repeat-seller match on the PAN/VAT and registration numbers ([07 TM-31](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#tm-31-fraudulent-vendor-onboarding-and-counterfeit-listings)), and `approveShopApplication` approves what the reviewer saw. The single-seller launch enters them in the profile ([12 §9](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#9-single-seller-launch-r1-s) part 1), and R1-S part 1b lets the owner change them on an `active` shop, audited, with the [04a §6.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#61-shops) CHECKs checked on the result ([06 note 64](06-api-design.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)); with other vendors, an approved shop could then put identifiers in place that nobody reviewed (found while building R1-S part 1b, 2026-11-23).                                                          | (a) Keep the R1-S rule for every shop: a direct edit, audited (`shop.profile_update`), with the CHECKs on the result. (b) The owner of an approved shop (`active` or `suspended`) may not change them (403 with a reason); staff change them with `adminUpdateShop` after checking new documents, and the owner edits them freely only while the shop is `pending_review` or `rejected`. (c) A change is held as a request that staff approve before it takes effect (a new table and queue).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | No recommendation yet. Tech-lead lean: (b), the smallest change that keeps TM-31's review meaningful; (c) only if vendors change these details often enough to need a queue. The owner's own shop moves to the same rule, or keeps (a) under OD-44's exception. **Safe default while open: (a), the R1-S build**, which is safe only while the owner's shop is the only shop; it is no default for M2.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Product owner (tech lead advises)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | M2 code (vendor onboarding: `updateShopProfile` and `adminUpdateShop`)                                                        | Before M2 approves the first shop other than the owner's                                                                                                        | Open                                                                   |
| OD-65 | Which S3-compatible server do development and CI run? The docs planned MinIO ([03 §3.5](03-system-architecture.md#35-object-storage-layout), [11 §2.2](11-deployment-and-operations.md), [ADR-0013](adr/0013-media-direct-upload-async-processing.md) decision 7), but MinIO stopped publishing its free container images in October 2025 and distributes its community edition as source only, as reported by Chainguard and Gigazine (03 §3.5, accessed 2026-11-24). Docker Compose had no S3 server and CI had none either; adding a CI service changes `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which needs a token with the `workflow` scope (found while building R1-S part 2d-i, 2026-11-24).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | (a) Pin the last published MinIO image by digest, if it can still be pulled: no further security updates, acceptable for a server bound to `127.0.0.1` and CI only. (b) A maintained rebuild of MinIO, such as Chainguard's free image (check its terms and tags). (c) Another S3-compatible server with published images, such as SeaweedFS or Garage (check licence, presigned-URL support and `Content-Type` signing). (d) A real R2 bucket for development and CI, with credentials in CI secrets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | **Decided 2026-11-25: (c), SeaweedFS** (§2.3), which passed the probe: it holds a presigned PUT to its signed `Content-Type` and refuses another type, as R2 does. Before the decision, the safe default was: the in-memory fake in the functional and concurrency suites and the S3 adapter's tests over a canned HTTP handler (10 §3.6, note 89); the photo operations answer 503 locally; the staging smoke of [11 §5](11-deployment-and-operations.md) checks a real upload against R2 before go-live.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Tech lead                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | The browser upload against a real store in development (R1-S part 2g) and in CI; not the build                                | Before R1-S part 2g's page check of a real upload; at the latest the staging smoke before go-live                                                               | **Decided**                                                            |

### 2.3 Decision log

Newest first. The entry format is in §1.5. A ruling that is not an OD but changes an owning document or an ADR is logged in the same format, headed "Not an OD".

```text
OD-23 — Re-decided 2026-11-26 by product owner (replaces the ruling of 2026-09-30)
Ruling: Option (b). DripNepal's code is proprietary, all rights reserved, and the repository
        stays public: LICENSE is an all-rights-reserved notice, package.json says "license":
        "UNLICENSED" (with "private": true), and openapi.yaml's info.license says the same.
        OD-22 (a) still holds, because the source is still public.
Evidence: The product owner's instruction of 2026-11-26: DripNepal is for their own use only,
          and the repository is public only for what GitHub gives public repositories free:
          CI minutes, branch protection on `main` and secret scanning. On GitHub Free a private
          repository loses the enforcement of `main`'s protection and has about 2,000 CI
          minutes a month (1,733 were used in the 30 days to 2026-11-26), and a paid plan is
          not possible for now. The change applies from the commit that records this ruling
          (2026-11-26): copies of earlier commits published under MIT keep that licence, and
          those commits stay in the public history (the repository had 0 forks and 0 stars on
          2026-11-26). Third-party code keeps its own licence (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and the
          LICENSE and NOTICE files in .agents/skills/). Making the repository private is to be
          revisited before go-live, or when a paid GitHub plan is possible.
ADR: ADR-0015 (dated note 2026-11-26; its revisit trigger fired, but no decision changed,
     because the kit's terms turn on the source being public).
Docs updated: §2.2, §3.1 (VX-13), §4.2 (R-15), §5, note 60, ADR-0015, 00, 01, 07, 08, 09, 12,
              README.md, openapi.yaml  |  Status: Decided → Resolved on 2026-11-26
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-26 by product owner: track the supabase-postgres-best-practices skill
Ruling: Supabase's supabase-postgres-best-practices skill is tracked as a fourth reviewed third-party
        skill, unchanged: .agents/skills/supabase-postgres-best-practices/ with a .claude/skills/
        symlink, ignored by Prettier, and listed in skills-lock.json and AGENTS.md. A NOTICE.md beside
        it records its source and where it disagrees with the documents, which win. It helps review
        SQL, indexes and locking; it decides nothing.
Evidence: Asked for by the product owner on 2026-11-26. Source: supabase/agent-skills, folder
          skills/supabase-postgres-best-practices/ at commit c9be0e9, copied verbatim (36 files).
          Licence: MIT, declared in its SKILL.md frontmatter and in the upstream repository's LICENSE
          (copyright 2026 Supabase), which is copied beside it because the folder has none.
          Read-through of all 36 files: Markdown only, no scripts, no `!` command run on load, no
          allowed-tools, no hooks and no instruction to fetch remote content; its URLs are PostgreSQL
          and Supabase documentation and GitHub compare and commit links in CHANGELOG.md. The
          `pnpm test` that references/_contributing.md mentions belongs to the upstream repository
          and is not copied. It is written for Supabase and disagrees with the documents on money
          (numeric, against ADR-0007), tenant isolation (row-level security, against 04 §2.5, 07 §1.5
          and ADR-0006), primary keys (bigint identity or the pg_uuidv7 extension, against 04 §2.1),
          pagination (cursors everywhere, against 06 §6), migrations (idempotent DO blocks, against
          09 §8), enum types (against 04 §2.4), queues (pg_cron, pgmq or a hand-made jobs table,
          against ADR-0010), connection settings (03 §3.4 and §8.2), roles and server settings
          (07 §4.10) and the search configuration (english, against ADR-0014's simple); NOTICE.md
          lists them. Its description asks to be loaded before any database change, so AGENTS.md
          names the first two. skills-lock.json's computedHash uses the algorithm of the 2026-11-09
          ncm-integration entry: SHA-256 over the code-point-sorted relative path, then content, of
          the 37 copied files (the 36 and LICENSE), NOTICE.md excluded.
ADR: none.
Docs updated: 09 (§1.5, note 133), 12 (E12, RF-42, note 83), AGENTS.md, note 59  |  Status: Decided
```

```text
OD-65 — Decided 2026-11-25 by product owner, on the tech lead's recommendation
Ruling: (c), SeaweedFS (Apache-2.0), pinned, in its all-in-one mini mode: the seaweedfs
        service of docker-compose.yml for development, which CI's Tests job also starts,
        so T-MED-005 runs the S3 adapter against a real S3 API. MinIO is not an option:
        it stopped publishing container images in October 2025, and its repository is
        reported archived in 2026 [Verify-external] (03 §3.5).
Evidence: A probe on 2026-11-25 against SeaweedFS 4.53: a presigned PUT is held to its
          signed Content-Type (another type or none: 403), as are its key and its expiry;
          a read with a stale If-Match is refused (412), also after the URL overwrote the
          object; HEAD gives the size, type and quoted ETag; a URL carrying the checksum
          of an empty body is refused for the real file; anonymous visitors read only the
          public buckets. T-MED-005 keeps these in CI. Behaviour that differs from R2 is
          still checked by the staging smoke.
ADR: none (a development and CI tool choice; ADR-0013 decision 7 and ADR-0016 carry dated
     edits).
Docs updated: 00 (§7.2, note 35), 03 (§3.5, §5.2, §11.1, note 41), 07 (§7.3, note 62),
              09 (§6.1, note 127), 10 (§3.2, §3.6, §6.5, §6.8, note 90), 11 (§1.9,
              §2.1–§2.3, §3.6, §4.3, §4.4, §5.2, §5.7, note 125), 12 (§4.1, §5.2, §9,
              note 77), ADR-0013, ADR-0016, risks (§2.2, §5, note 58)
              |  Status: Decided → Resolved on 2026-11-25
```

```text
Ruling: (b) with the single_operator_mode exception. Platform staff may own or work in a
        shop. A platform action on their own shop or its orders answers 403, unless it is
        done under the single-operator exception, which needs a fresh totp_code and writes

        action names that apply it are designed with R1-S parts 1 and 6 and stay (proposed).

        (A-20), so R1-S runs with single_operator_mode = true, set at go-live (12 §9 part 7).
        No refund, payout or adjustment operation ships in R1-S, so OD-14's self-approval

        note 54), 12 (§9, note 68), risks (§2.2, §5, note 53).
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-23 by product owner: launch first as a single-seller store (R1-S)
Ruling: DripNepal launches as soon as possible as a single-seller store, release stage R1-S
        (01 §5.5): the product owner is the only seller, through their own shop, and adds
        features and later other vendors after launch. The data model stays multi-vendor
        and R1 (M0 to M7) stays the full target. In scope: the storefront (browse, category
        listings, search, filters, product details with variant choice, cart), guest
        checkout with cash on delivery (entry below), tracking an order by its number; the
        owner's shop and seller area, a web product editor (products, variants, prices,
        stock, photos, publish), the order list and detail, accept and cancel, Nepal Can
        Move booking and tracking, and delivered, COD collected and returned. Deferred until
        after launch, not cancelled: vendor applications and review, KYC, other shops'
        onboarding, payouts, commission and the ledger, vendor remittances, product
        moderation, refunds automation (manual at first), support cases (phone and email at
        first), reviews, wishlist, coupons, the wallet gateway (M8), M1 part 7 (admin user
        pages, suspension, revealUserContact) and the courier admin pages 3b-ii and 3c. The
        courier phases 4 and 5 (the seller Courier section and the booking dialog) fold
        into the launch's seller logistics part. Selling the owner's own goods conflicts
        with the 01 §5.4 non-goal "DripNepal-owned stock" and REG-01; OD-63 asks counsel for
        the legal posture, and it blocks go-live, not the build.
Evidence: R1 is 40 to 70 ideal developer-weeks (12 §3), and M1 parts 1 to 6b and the courier
          path of M6 are built. An earlier single-seller v1 (entry of 2026-11-03 below) was
          a separate prototype line never hosted; this launch is built on main, to these
          documents.
ADR: none (release scope and schedule; no structural change).
Docs updated: 00 (§1.3, §2.1, §2.3, §2.4, §3.2, §3.4, §6, §7.1 to §7.3, §8, note 30),
              01 (§1.3, §3.1, §4.7, §5.1 to §5.5, §6, §11.1, FR-CHK-001, FR-CHK-007,
              FR-ORD-001, FR-ORD-009, REG-01, note 36), 02 (§3, J-05, note 30),
              03 (D7, note 38), 04 (note 43), 04a (§7.6, §11.1), 05 (§3.3, §4.1, §7.3,
              note 35), 06 (§13.4, note 62), 07 (TM-23, §4.6, note 54), 08 (§2.2, note 85), 10 (§5.2, note 81),
              12 (§1, §2.7, §3, §5.1, §5.6, §9, note 68), risks (introduction, §2.1,
              §2.2 OD-63, §3.1 VX-16, §3.2, §4.2 R-02, R-12 and R-21, §5, note 53)
              |  Status: Decided
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-23 by product owner: guest checkout with cash on delivery (revises Q6)
Ruling: A guest may place a COD order with a name, a required Nepal mobile number and an
        optional email; signed-in checkout stays. This revises the Q6 answer of 2026-09-25
        ("checkout requires sign-in with a verified email and a Nepal mobile number").
        Fraud controls: a limit on open orders per phone number and on guest orders per
        client network [Assumption A-60, values configurable]; and the seller confirms each
        guest order by phone before booking the courier, recorded with
        orders.verification_status and verification_method = phone_call (OD-57, A-55).
        A buyer tracks an order on a public page with the order number plus the phone
        number, because order numbers alone can be guessed; the confirmation email, when an
        email was given, may carry a link with a signed token instead. The detailed rules
        (05 placeOrder, the 04a guest columns, the 06 operations, the 07 ownership and limit
        rules, the 10 test IDs) are written with launch parts 4 and 5 (12 §9).
Evidence: The product owner wants buyers to order without creating an account. Phone OTP
          stays R2 (FR-IAM-010), so the phone confirmation before booking is the check on
          a guest's number (R-02).
ADR: none.
Docs updated: as the entry above  |  Status: Decided
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-23 by product owner: the delivery fee is Nepal Can Move's live rate
Ruling: Checkout charges NCM's live rate for the buyer's address, from the shop's pickup
        branch to the destination branch that serves the address's district. When NCM does
        not answer with a usable rate, checkout uses a fallback fee the seller sets
        [Assumption A-61]: proposed as the shop's existing zone rates (FR-SHOP-004), fixed
        at placement like every fee (05 §3.3), which the phone confirmation never raises.
        An "estimate the phone call settles" would change an order total after placement,
        which 05 §3.3 and Directive 2082 s8(3) [Verify-external VX-02] do not allow today;
        the owner may choose it, with a 05 and 04a change. Mapping the buyer's district to
        an NCM destination branch name is the open design point of launch part 4.
Evidence: The rate call was observed on NCM's demo host on 2026-11-23 (VX-16 row, §3.1):
          its shape and failure modes are known, production rates and branches are not.
ADR: none.
Docs updated: as the first entry of 2026-11-23 above  |  Status: Decided
```

```text
OD-58 — Re-decided 2026-11-22 by product owner, for R1 (replaces the ruling of 2026-11-21)
Ruling: Option (b). R1 offers a platform-owned NCM account to shops that have no active
        account of their own: staff allow each shop on it, and a shop's own active account
        wins (`replaceLogisticsSettings` accepts `platform` only for a shop without one).
        NCM remits the COD of those parcels to DripNepal, which reconciles each parcel by hand
        and pays the vendor, net of NCM's charges, outside the ledger in R1. Platform mode is
        built in M6, but live COD on a platform-owned account stays off
        (`platform_account_cod_enabled` (proposed) false) until counsel clears REG-27 and VX-01,
        the accountant approves the records, NCM answers VX-17 and VX-18, and the M7 ledger's
        treatment of these parcels is set.
Evidence: The product owner's instruction of 2026-11-22 ("we will allow vendors to use platform
          ncm account if they don't have their own ncm while supporting their own ncm key as
          well") and answers to the follow-up questions (COD to DripNepal; staff allow each
          shop; own account wins). Owner answers on VX-16 (no cancellation API; a comment asks
          NCM to cancel), VX-17 (one account serves many vendors) and VX-18 (COD to DripNepal).
ADR: ADR-0019 (dated note 2026-11-22; decision 8 and the revisit trigger for ADR-0009).
Docs updated: §2.1, §2.2, §3.1 (VX-16, VX-17, VX-18), §4.2 (R-33), §5, note 45, ADR-0019,
              ADR-0009, 00, 01, 02, 03, 04, 04a, 05, 06, 07, 10, 12, openapi.yaml,
              research (ncm-reference-integration)
              |  Status: Decided
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-22 by product owner: the sources of the locations dataset
Ruling: The locations seeder is built from Nepal Post's federal postal-code list as the Postal
        Directorate Biratnagar publishes it in HTML (codes, ward counts, types, Nepali names), the
        Central Bureau of Statistics' "Geographical Codes for States, Districts and Local Bodies"
        on Open Data Nepal (English names, joined by the same codes), and the Survey Department's
        boundaries on HDX for local levels renamed since the CBS list. A hand-checked corrections
        file holds the renames and fixes, and the owner accepted the judgement calls listed to
        them (Jaimini, Limchungbung, Manang Ngisyang, Dodhara Chandani, CBS district spellings,
        Nepal Post's Nepali spellings).
Evidence: The Nepal Post PDF host closed the connection and mofaga.gov.np timed out or refused it
          from the build session, by two network routes (2026-11-22). The HTML list matches every
          count of 04 §21.4; all 753 codes and 77 district codes match the CBS list; 50 Nepali
          names differ, 37 of them local levels renamed since the CBS list.
ADR: none.
Docs updated: 04 (§20.3, §21.4, §21.5, note 29), 09 (§1.2, §1.4, §8.8, note 107), 10 (§6.2,
              note 63), 12 (§4.1, §4.3, §5.6, note 48), 00 (§8, §9.6, note 25), 01 (§11.1, note 28), VX-10,
```

```text
OD-61 — Decided 2026-11-21 by product owner
Ruling: Option (a). The NCM adapter sends `merchant_reference` without its hyphens as `vref_id`
        (`DN-4K7Q2M9-1-S1` becomes `DN4K7Q2M91S1`); the fixed 7-character order code and the `S`
        keep it unique, and an R1 reference is at most 14 characters. A reference longer than 15
        characters is refused before the call. `shipments.merchant_reference` and its CHECK do not
        change; staff search NCM's portal by the hyphenless form after an `unknown` booking.

          before any call).
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-09 by product owner: commit the reviewed ncm-integration skill
Ruling: The ncm-integration skill that commit bd23da5 added to main under .claude/skills/ is tracked as
        a third-party skill, unchanged: it moves to .agents/skills/ncm-integration/ with a
        .claude/skills/ symlink, is ignored by Prettier, and is listed in skills-lock.json and AGENTS.md.
        A NOTICE.md beside it records its source and that the documents win where it disagrees. It
        guides the M6 NCM adapter; it decides nothing.
Evidence: Licence: MIT (its LICENSE, copyright 2026 harublank0-0). Read-through of SKILL.md and the four
          references/*.md files: no scripts, no `!` command run on load, no allowed-tools, no hooks, and
          no instruction to fetch remote content; its URLs are NCM's demo host and an example domain.
          Its API facts cite NCM documents (an API PDF dated 251222, a webhook doc v1.1) and one
          production integration, none of which is in this repository, and label them [doc],
          [observed], [reported], [inferred] or [unknown]. They are not verified here: VX-16 stays
          open and ADR-0019 decision 6 stays Proposed. Where it differs from the documents (its stage
          names against 05 §6.3, decimal-string money against ADR-0007, its decisions D1 to D11
          against OD-56, OD-58 and ADR-0019), the documents win. skills-lock.json records this
          repository as its source, because no upstream repository is known, and NOTICE.md names
          commit bd23da5; its
          computedHash is SHA-256 over the sorted relative paths and contents of the six skill files,
          which may not be the algorithm of the two older entries (never checked, 2026-11-04 entry).
ADR: none.
Docs updated: 09 (§1.5, note 85), 12 (E12, note 24), AGENTS.md, note 28  |  Status: Decided
Edited 2026-11-11: the skill is no longer unchanged. At the product owner's request its NCM API facts
        follow NCM's API Documentation v2 (a 32-page PDF NCM sent to the product owner, kept out of the
        repository), which replaces the PDF dated 251222: new limits, webhook registration and test,
        assigned branches, ticket detail and reply, customers, the cross-NCM customer rating, label
        data, and a new decision D12 on that rating. Its NOTICE.md lists the changes. The facts are
        still unverified: the demo token NCM issued answered "Invalid token." on 2026-11-11, so VX-16,
        VX-17 and VX-18 stay open, and the documents still win. skills-lock.json's computedHash is
        recomputed with the algorithm above. Docs updated: 09 (§1.5, note 86), note 30.
Edited 2026-11-16: the product owner's repository harublank0-0/ncm-integration-skill is now the
        skill's upstream. Its skills/ncm-integration/ folder at commit a1d6ba3 is copied verbatim over
        this copy: SKILL.md and the four existing references change, and references/open-questions.md
        is added. The local v2 changes of 2026-11-11 are in the upstream, so none remain.
        skills-lock.json names the upstream as source, and its computedHash is recomputed with the
        algorithm above (now seven files). Read-through of all seven copied files: no scripts, no `!`
        command run on load, no allowed-tools, no hooks and no instruction to fetch remote content;
        open-questions.md mentions the upstream's scripts/build_reference.py, which is not copied.
        As a verbatim copy, the skill still calls the production host unknown; the read-only
        observation of the same day is recorded in the VX-16 row (§3.1) and belongs in the upstream
        next. VX-16, VX-17 and VX-18 stay open, and the documents still win.
        Docs updated: 09 (§1.5, note 94), VX-16, note 34.
Edited 2026-11-19: the copy is refreshed, verbatim, from the upstream at commit 72a6ce4 (its pull
        requests #2 to #4, merged 2026-11-16): SKILL.md and the five references change, and no file
        is added or removed. They add a label [observed 2026-11-16] for what 26 read-only calls to
        NCM's production API returned that day (the production host, the branch-list and rate
        shapes, `vendor_return` sent as the string "False", money as strings and as a JSON number,
        no rate-limit headers), and [reported] findings from the reference integration's production
        data (undocumented callback events, repeated vref_id values accepted, comment ids).
        Read-through of the changes: no scripts, no `!` command run on load, no allowed-tools, no
        hooks and no instruction to fetch remote content. skills-lock.json's computedHash is
        recomputed with the algorithm above. NCM has confirmed none of it, nothing that books or
        changes data was called, and the demo token was not reissued: VX-16, VX-17 and VX-18 stay
        open, and the documents still win. Docs updated: 09 (§1.5, note 96), VX-16, note 37.
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-07 by product owner: the E4 baseline lands through an integration branch
Ruling: The E4 baseline is built on the integration branch `feat/e4-schema-baseline`. Its parts (the
        baseline files, the models and the constraint tests) arrive there in small pull requests, each
        reviewed with the baseline's migration and constraint tests passing; the full CI gate applies
        to the merge into `main`. `main` keeps the v1 store's code working until the branch is
        complete: all 14 baseline files, and the v1 code that uses the old models adapted or removed.
        The branch then merges into `main` in one pull request. This answers the staging question of
        the 2026-11-04 entry and note 24.
Evidence: The baseline deletes the v1 migrations and replaces the models under the same names, and over
          60 files import `#models`, so a partial baseline on `main` would break the v1 store and its CI.
          The team has reached Linear's free-tier issue limit, so the baseline has no Linear issues and its
          branches are named outside the Linear rule (09 §11.6).
ADR: none.

              note 21), AGENTS.md  |  Status: Decided
```

```text
OD-57 — Decided 2026-11-05 by product owner
Ruling: Option (c). Platform-level customer verification is a flag on `orders`: `verification_status`
        (`pending`, `verified`, `review_required`, `failed`), `verification_method`, `verified_at` and
        `verification_notes`. There is no verification state machine and no hold: vendors see, accept
        and pack an order whatever its verification status. Future business rule: a shop order whose
        parent is `review_required` or `failed` is not dispatched (no courier booking, no `shipped`)
        until verification is resolved; the guard is built with the M6 dispatch actions.
Evidence: Q6 checks the account only; phone OTP stays R2 (FR-IAM-010). Fake COD orders cost vendors a
          courier trip both ways (R-02).
ADR: none (a column set and a business rule).
Docs updated: §2.2, 00 (A-55, §7.2, §7.3), 01 (FR-ORD-009, §4.7, §5.2, §6), 12 (§4.1 E4, §5.6)  |  Status: Decided
              Data model, same day: 04a §11.1 (columns, `orders_guard` list), 04 (INV-33), 05 §6.3
              (dispatch rule), 07 (permission map), 02 (AC-J12-21), 10 §6 (T-ORD-011, T-ORD-109)
```

```text
OD-56 — Decided 2026-11-05 by product owner
Ruling: Option (c) with a reduced scope. Logistics is an R1 capability. R1 has a courier registry that
        depends on no one provider (Nepal Can Move first), logistics accounts owned by the platform or
        by a shop, the shops allowed to use each account, per-shop logistics settings, several pickup
        addresses per shop, shipments that keep the account and pickup address used, shipment items
        and courier tracking events. The tables join the E4 baseline; booking and tracking through the
        courier API are built in M6, and vendor-entered fulfilment stays as the `manual` mode. One
        shipment per shop order stays in R1. Out of R1: choosing between couriers, automated COD
        settlement, vendor payouts and courier returns; the ledger is not a prerequisite. COD on
        platform-owned accounts is reconciled by hand and waits for OD-58. NCM's support for pickups
        from many vendors on one account and for central COD remittance is not assumed (VX-17, VX-18).
        Credentials are stored encrypted in the database (ADR-0019). FR-FUL-004 is Must, so the R1
        launch waits for the NCM adapter (VX-16) unless the product owner records here a change of
        FR-FUL-004 to Should with a dated plan (01 §1.3). The product owner moved nothing out of R1
        for this addition; the M6 effort grows instead (R-12 fired, R-33 added).
Evidence: The product owner treats delivery as essential to the first marketplace release. NCM has an
          API for registered vendors, but its documentation is private (VX-16).
ADR: ADR-0019 (Accepted for the schema; Proposed for the adapter until VX-16 closes); dated note on
     ADR-0009.
Docs updated: §1.2, §2.2, §3.1, §3.2, §4.2 (R-12, R-33), §5, 00 (§2.1, §2.3, §3.2, §3.3, §3.4, A-28,
              A-53, A-54, §7.2, §7.3, §8), 01 (§2.4, §2.5, §4.1, §4.3, §4.5, §5.1, §5.2, §5.4, §6,
              FR-FUL-004, §11.1), 03 (§1.1, §2, §4.4, §11.1, §14), 12 (§3, §4.1, §5.6, §6), AGENTS.md,
              ADR-0019, adr/README  |  Status: Decided
              Data model, same day: 04a (§6.6, §9.8 to §9.11, §11.5, §11.6, §11.9), 04 (INV-28 to
              INV-32, §16.4, §19.3, §20.2.2, §21.6), 05 (§6.3, §7.12), 07 (TB-11, TM-32, two proposed
              permissions), 02 (J-12, J-13), 06 and openapi.yaml (`ShipmentStatus`), 10 §6 (test
              IDs), 12 (M0 and M6 exits)
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-11-04 by product owner: a Fable code review before every pull request
Ruling: Before a pull request is opened, a Fable-model reviewer reviews the commit it proposes. The author
        checks each finding, fixes the real ones (fix commits are reviewed too) and lists the findings and
        their outcome in the description. A Claude Code PreToolUse hook (.claude/settings.json and
        .claude/hooks/) blocks the GitHub create_pull_request tool and `gh pr create` until a review of
        that commit is recorded in the clone's git directory.
Evidence: A Fable review of the v1 store on 2026-11-04 found defects that the tests and CI had not, among
          them a lost stock update on product edit and the buyer's contact details on order tracking.
ADR: none.
Docs updated: 09 §11.5 and note 78, AGENTS.md  |  Status: Decided
```

```text

Ruling: PR #142 merged the v1 branch into main, so main now carries the single-seller v1 store as well as
        the documents. Production deploys from main: a release is a commit on main with green CI, tagged
        vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH, and the server checks out the tag. M0 continues on main in pull requests that
        keep the store working; the steps that cannot (the E4 baseline of ADR-0011 with the OD-55 data
        import) land together at one planned cutover release. The v1 branch is retired. This replaces
        the branch split of the 2026-11-03 entry below.
Evidence: #142 merged v1 into main on 2026-11-04; DEPLOY.md already cloned and pulled the default branch.
ADR: ADR-0011 (dated note, 2026-11-04): its baseline merge is also the live store's cutover.
Docs updated: DEPLOY.md (Releasing, Updating), .github/workflows/ci.yml, 10 §5 and note 30, ADR-0011
              Status, the dripnepal-testing skill, risks note 24  |  Status: Decided
Edited 2026-11-07: there is no live store to cut over. v1 was never hosted and every database row is seed
        data (OD-55 entry above), so the baseline release carries no data import, and "the live store's
        cutover" above describes a store that never went live. M0 pull requests still keep the v1 store's
        code on `main` working: the baseline lands through an integration branch (entry above, 2026-11-07).
```

```text
Ruling: (6) The server computes every refund line amount (05 §3.3, §7.4); clients send lines and quantities, never
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-10-01 by tech lead: who owns and migrates the pgboss schema
Ruling: dripnepal_migrator owns the pgboss schema. pg-boss's own schema install or upgrade runs in the release
        container as the migrator, which re-grants USAGE on pgboss and DML on its tables to dripnepal_app after
        every install or upgrade. Both pg-boss instances run as dripnepal_app (the worker's and the web send-only
        one) and start with pg-boss's migration disabled; dripnepal_app has DML only.
Evidence: 07 §4.10 gives dripnepal_app no DDL, while pg-boss creates and upgrades its schema at start unless told
          not to (11 note 32). The pg-boss 12 option and the schema-step call stay pseudocode until the M0 spike
          T-ARCH-004 (proposed) confirms them (03 §10.1 item 5).
ADR: ADR-0010 decision 1 and ADR-0011 decision 2 (dated notes, 2026-10-01).

              ADR-0010, ADR-0011 (2026-10-01)  |  Status: Decided; Resolved when the M0 release step and the
              pg-boss start follow it
Edited 2026-11-11: T-ARCH-004 confirmed both. The runtime instances start with migrate, createSchema,
        persistQueueStats and reindex off; the step is node ace jobs:install, which installs or upgrades the
        schema, waits for the upgrade's out-of-transaction commands, then grants (ADR-0010). Note 32.
```

```text
Not an OD — Decided 2026-10-01 by tech lead: strong ETags for versioned resources
Ruling: Versioned resources return the strong tag ETag: "<version>" (no W/ prefix). If-Match uses strong
        comparison (RFC 9110 §13.1.1): only "<version>" equal to the current version passes; a weak tag or any
        other value gives 412 VERSION_CONFLICT with the current representation; a missing header, * or a list
        stays 428 PRECONDITION_REQUIRED. The version is treated as a strong validator of the resource's editable
        state [Assumption].
Evidence: RFC 9110 §13.1.1 requires strong comparison for If-Match (06 note 11).
ADR: ADR-0004 decision 3 (dated note, 2026-10-01).
Docs updated: 01 AC-FR-CAT-011-1, 02 J-10, 06 §3.6, §8, §14.2 and §15, 09 §4.1 and §12, 10 §6.3 T-API-004,
              openapi.yaml (the ETag header, the IfMatch parameter, the 412 and 428 responses and the examples;
              its note 22), ADR-0004 (2026-10-01)  |  Status: Decided; Resolved when the first versioned
              endpoint is built
```

OD-01, OD-12, OD-13, OD-23 and OD-25 were Decided on 2026-09-30, and the documents named in their entries were updated the same day. OD-47 was Decided on 2026-10-01, and the documents named in its entry follow it. Five rulings of the same day that are not ODs are logged above it, and above those the 17 rulings that the product owner and the tech lead accepted in the cross-document check of the same day, numbered as in that check and grouped in five entries by area; each entry names any document that still has to follow. The entry of 2026-10-04 comes from the final review of the consistency review: shop-application decisions are checked against the version the reviewer loaded. Each OD stays Decided until the work named in its "Resolved when" clause is done, because Resolved (§1.3) also needs the code updated. Later decisions are logged above these entries, newest first. Rows marked Proposed are already used as defaults across the docs set, so approving one only means changing its status and adding its log entry.

## 3. External verification register (VX-01 … VX-15)

### 3.1 Register

Evidence is quoted from the [research notes](research/README.md). Every URL was accessed on 2026-09-25. **Contradictions** found in the sources are marked as such; each must be settled by the verifier, not by picking the more convenient source.

                           | Current evidence (sources accessed 2026-09-25)                                                                                                                                           | Verifier                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Blocks | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| VX-01 | Is it NRB-licensed activity for a marketplace to collect customer payments for third-party vendors into its own merchant account and pay them later? Is a delayed vendor settlement or any held balance e-money?                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Payment and Settlement Act 2075 s5: no PSO or PSP activity without an NRB licence. This is quoted in NRB's NPS Master Reference Document (Oct 2025, https://www.nrb.org.np/contents/uploads/2025/10/National-Payment-Switch-NPS-and-the-National-Payment-Ecosystem-Master-Reference-Document-2025.pdf). The same document says third-party aggregators "aggregate all of their clients' transactions into a single merchant account" and that NRB "may make" provisions for them, so they are not yet provided for. The NRB Oversight Report 2024/25 flags a "PSP … offering e-commerce platform services through its wallet" (https://www.nrb.org.np/contents/uploads/2026/08/Payment-Oversight-Report-2024-25-1.pdf). Under the Unified Directive on Payment Systems 2082, e-money is issued only against a settlement account. eSewa and Khalti are licensed PSPs (NRB list as of 2083/03/32, https://www.nrb.org.np/psd/licensed-list-of-payment-system-operator-pso-and-payment-service-provider-psp-9/). Neither documents a split-payment or sub-merchant feature. **Gap:** no official English text of PSA s5 (the NRB PDF is a scan).
