| VX-02 | Which E-Commerce Act 2081 and Directive 2082 obligations bind DripNepal as an intermediary? Sub-questions: does a one-way salted password hash (scrypt, 01 REG-14) meet the Directive s8(1) "encrypted form" wording? When does the 7-day refund deadline of Directive s9(3) start?                                                                                                                                                | Act in force from the 31st day after assent on 16 Mar 2025 (English translation, https://giwmscdnone.gov.np/media/files/E-Commerce%20Act%2C%202081_yr7k9o5.pdf). The Act covers: s4(2) disclosures incl. listing number, updated within 48 h; s5 DoCSCP listing; s6 per-listing disclosures; s14 intermediary duties (seller agreement before listing, seller neutrality, returns regardless of contract); s16 seller documents; s33 complaint decided within 15 days; s22 fines Rs 20,000–1,00,000 and s23 fines Rs 50,000–5,00,000 or 6 months–3 years. Directive 2082 (https://giwmscdnone.gov.np/media/pdf_upload/ecommerce-directives_8errkt4.pdf) covers: s4(3) listing attachments (privacy and return policies, domain proof, e-invoice permission, test report); s8(1)–(3); s9(1)–(3); s14 records for ≥ 5 years. Enforcement is active (DoCSCP Notice 06/083-84, https://doc.gov.np/content/470/urgent-notice-regarding-mandatory-listing-e-commerce-2083-05-01/). **Open points:** must each vendor list separately; labelling of sponsored listings (s14(d)); s8(1) says passwords are stored "encrypted", while the research fact-check recommends a one-way hash; s9(3) gives no start point for the 7-day refund (the design uses approval + 7 days, 05 §11). No retention, transfer or breach-notice clause, per secondary sources (DLA Piper, updated 20 Mar 2026, https://www.dlapiperdataprotection.com/?t=law&c=NP). **Gap:** full text of Privacy Regulation 2077 not found. consent, s10(2) exempts government notices; s9(1) ads state the advertiser's name and address, s9(2) any required warning; s25(3) fine up to Rs 1 lakh for breaching ss7–12; the text includes the Some Nepal Laws Amendment Act 2082. **Gap:** no English translation checked; channels other than email and SMS are not addressed. | VX-05 | VAT rate and registration, invoices, e-billing thresholds, TDS and advance tax. Sub-question: how is reverse-charge VAT (VAT Act s8(2)) assessed and filed on USD or EUR invoices for hosting, email and SaaS bought from abroad (see VX-15, R-32)?                                                                                                                                                                                | VAT 13%, with multiple rates up to 13% allowed by Finance Act 2083 (IRD booklet https://ird.gov.np/content/13632/economic-act--2083---information-booklet-on/). Registration thresholds Rs 50 lakh for goods, Rs 30 lakh for services or mixed (VAT Rules r6(1); VAT Act s11, https://ird.gov.np/content/13407/value-added-tax-act--2052--as-amended/). Unregistered sellers may not show tax (s15). Tax fraction 13/113 (r18(7); the official text prints "×" where "+" is meant). **Contradiction:** mandatory e-invoicing starts above Rs 20 crore turnover (IRD notice, 4 Baishakh 2083, https://ird.gov.np/content/13488/cbms-notice-01-04/), but a Rs 10 crore threshold is reported for the FY 2083/84 budget (secondary, https://english.clickmandu.com/2026/05/9187/). Treat Rs 20 crore as operative until IRD issues a notice. Electronic Invoice Procedure 2082 s6–s7: append-only records, server in Nepal (https://ird.gov.np/category/electronic-invoice/). Income Tax Act (https://ird.gov.np/content/13405/income-tax-act--2058--as-amended-by/): s95A(6e) 1% advance tax; s88 TDS 15% or 1.5%; s89 1.5% on contracts over Rs 50,000. VAT s8(2) reverse charge on foreign services; s25(1kha) 10% VAT refund on e-payments. Cash transaction limit Rs 25,000 (Finance Act 2083). | VX-06 | eSewa capabilities: verification, webhooks, refunds, expiry, credentials.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Sources: https://developer.esewa.com.np/pages/Epay, …/pages/Intent, …/pages/Test-credentials. ePay v2 is a browser form POST with an HMAC-SHA256 signature over `total_amount,transaction_uuid,product_code` and a signed Base64 redirect; **gap:** the query parameter name is not documented. The status API returns `COMPLETE`, `PENDING`, `FULL_REFUND`, `PARTIAL_REFUND`, `AMBIGUOUS`, `NOT_FOUND` or `CANCELED`, or the error "Service is currently unavailable". No webhook, no refund API. The payment fails if not paid within 5 minutes of login. The Intent flow has a signed server callback, but only development URLs are published. **Contradictions:** the test password is `Nepal@123` on the Epay page but `Test@123` on the Test Credentials page; the production status host is `esewa.com.np` in the docs but `epay.esewa.com.np` in a third-party plugin. Live credentials are issued only after successful test transactions. Merchant documents: https://blog.esewa.com.np/documents-required-esewa-merchant-api-integration A server-side initiate returns `pidx`, `payment_url`, `expires_at` and `expires_in`. The `return_url` redirect is unsigned. For the lookup API, "only Completed" counts as success; Pending means hold. No webhook. The amount must exceed Rs 10. Payments above NPR 200 fail until merchant KYC is complete. Refunds are netted against future collections. **Contradictions:** link expiry is "60 minutes in production (default)", but the sample shows `expires_in` 1800 (30 min), so use `expires_at` from each response. The refund page gives no HTTP method or auth header, and its example "50.00" conflicts with "paisa". **Gap:** handling of a duplicate `purchase_order_id` is undocumented. calendar that Node 24 lacks, and 7 years is always at least as long (cost: up to one extra year of storage). 05 §5.11 rule 5 cites this schedule too. `audit_logs` and financial tables follow that schedule, and the audit rows of access and administration actions are kept 7 years like financial actions (proposed; 00 A-34); the retention command purges append-only tables through the proposed `forbid_mutation()` retention exemption, which needs the product owner's approval (00 A-35, [07 §4.10](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#410-database-roles-and-grants)); no hard deletes before the retention period ends; anonymisation of non-transactional PII (FR-IAM-009); archives beyond DigitalOcean's 7-day backups (R-29). | VX-09 | May DripNepal host outside Nepal?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | DCCS Directive 2081 (translation https://giwmscdnone.gov.np/media/pdf_upload/data%20center%20translation_bhnhhri.pdf): cl. 3(1) providers must be DoIT-listed; cl. 8(1) "any client shall only obtain" services from listed providers, and **"client" is undefined**; cl. 8(3) notify NCSC. | VX-10 | Which location dataset is authoritative for provinces, districts, local levels and wards?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | MoFAGA lists 7 provinces and 77 districts (https://mofaga.gov.np/local-contact/dcc-prov-1). The Nepal Post federal code list (https://giwmscdnone.gov.np/media/pdf_upload/Postal%20Code_wteggid.pdf) gives 753 local levels (6 metropolitan, 11 sub-metropolitan, 276 municipalities, 460 rural municipalities) and 6,743 wards, with up to 33 wards per local level (Pokhara). Nawalparasi and Rukum are split across provinces. Some codes are special counters, not wards. Imported 2026-11-22 (04 §21.4, §2.3): Nepal Post's list as the Postal Directorate Biratnagar publishes it, with English names from the Central Bureau of Statistics under the same codes and hand-checked corrections (45 at import, 54 after the MoFAGA cross-check); every count above holds. Cross-checked 2026-11-22 against Internet Archive copies of MoFAGA's 2026 directory: 734 of 753 local levels covered, 572 Nepali names identical, 12 clear Nepal Post typos corrected | VX-16 | Nepal Can Move merchant API: how it authenticates (token per merchant account?), whether it books a shipment and returns a waybill or tracking number, how status updates arrive (signed callback or polling only), whether it cancels a booking, what it needs to identify the pickup and destination (branch codes?), its rate limits and whether a sandbox exists.                                                              | No public documentation. Edited 2026-11-11: NCM's API document v2 (a PDF NCM gave the product owner, kept out of the repository) and webhook document v1.1 describe token authentication (`Authorization: Token`), an order create that returns NCM's order ID, status reads, booking by branch name, unsigned callbacks that are never retried, and daily limits; they offer no cancellation and no lookup by our reference. The `ncm-integration` skill records them. None is verified: the demo token NCM issued answered "Invalid token." on 2026-11-11. **Gap:** the production host, cancellation, callback authentication beyond a URL secret, the branch-list and rate response shapes, and a working demo token. Edited 2026-11-16: observed by the product owner's session, not confirmed by NCM. Read-only calls with the product owner's production token answered HTTP 200 on `https://portal.nepalcanmove.com` with `Authorization: Token <key>`: `GET /api/v2/vendor/staffs` (a paged object whose items carry `pk`, not the documented `id`) and `GET /api/v2/branches` (a plain list of branches with `pk`, `code`, `name`, `district_name`, `province_name`, `areas_covered`, `branch_type`, `surcharge` and contact fields). This narrows the production-host and branch-list gaps. Cancellation, callback authentication, the rate response shape, rate limits and booking stay unverified, and the demo token was not reissued. Edited 2026-11-19: the `ncm-integration` skill, at upstream commit `72a6ce4`, records 26 read-only calls of 2026-11-16 with one vendor's production token, observed and not confirmed by NCM: the rate answers `{"charge": "<decimal string>"}`, takes branch names only and fails with HTTP 200 (an HTML page, or `"0"` for an unknown `type`); the order detail sends `vendor_return` as the string `"False"`; responses carry no rate-limit headers. This narrows the rate-shape gap. Cancellation, callback authentication, rate limits and booking stay unverified, and the demo token was not reissued. Edited 2026-11-19, later: NCM issued a working demo token, and the unwired adapter was run against `https://demo.nepalcanmove.com`, observed and not confirmed by NCM. Its reads answered as the adapter expects (assigned branches, bulk statuses, parcel detail, status history), except that branch rows carry `district_name` and `areas_covered`, as on production, which the adapter did not read until `T-FUL-109`'s branch case was added. One booking succeeded: `POST /api/v1/order/create` answered 200 with `Message` and `orderid` plus undocumented `weight`, `delivery_charge` (a JSON number), `regular_delivery_charge`, `delivery_discount` and `delivery_type` (`"Pickup/Collect"` for a `Door2Door` booking); `cod_charge` `"100.00"` was accepted; a `vref_id` of 16 characters was refused with `400 {"Message": {}, "Error": {"vref_id": …}}` ("Vendor Reference ID Too Long"; `Message` is a string on success) and 15 accepted, which [OD-61](#22-decision-table) takes up; `orderid` and `weight` came as JSON numbers. The parcel's detail and history matched the production shapes. This narrows the booking gap; cancellation, callback authentication, rate limits and NCM's confirmation stay open. Edited 2026-11-20: the product owner sent NCM the open questions and recorded, in [research: ncm-reference-integration](research/ncm-reference-integration.md), what the NCM integration of another platform of theirs reports from production, not confirmed by NCM: webhooks carry no signature (only `User-Agent: NCM-Webhook/1.0`), parcels keep moving after a cancel comment, nothing finds a parcel by `vref_id` or refuses a repeated one, and `vendor_return` was always a string. VX-16 stays open until NCM answers. Edited 2026-11-21: the wired M6 path was run against the demo host, observed and not confirmed by NCM. The account's test call (assigned branches: `TINKUNE`) made it `active`; the branch sync read 6 branches with their districts; the booking job decrypted the delivery address and booked order 6302 as `DN-0000006-1-S1` (sent as `DN00000061S1`), and an adapter-level booking of the longest R1 shape, `DN-VX16803-12-S10` (14 characters without hyphens), was accepted as order 6301; a 16-character reference was refused by the adapter before any call. The history (`Pickup Order Created`), parcel detail (COD and delivery charge in two-decimal strings, `vendor_return` false) and bulk statuses parsed as the adapter expects; the poll stored the label once and nothing on a second run. Still unverified: cancellation, callback authentication, rate limits, a callback from NCM itself (it needs a public URL), production booking, and NCM's confirmation of all of it. Edited 2026-11-22: the product owner confirms that NCM has no cancellation API: DripNepal leaves a comment on the order and NCM's staff cancel it [Confirmed]. A comment is a request, not a stop: another integration saw parcels keep moving after one ([research: ncm-reference-integration](research/ncm-reference-integration.md)), so how quickly NCM acts, and how to stop a pickup, stay open with callback authentication, rate limits, a callback from NCM itself, production booking and NCM's confirmation of the rest. Edited 2026-11-23: the rate call was observed on NCM's demo host on 2026-11-23, read-only with the demo token; it stays [Verify-external VX-16] until NCM confirms it and it is checked on production. `GET /api/v1/shipping-rate?creation=&destination=&type=` takes branch **names** (the demo has 6: `BUTWAL`, `DAMAK`, `JANAKPUR`, `POKHARA`, `SANKHU`, `TINKUNE`; the account's assigned pickup branch is `TINKUNE`) and answers JSON in about 0.35 s: `TINKUNE` to `POKHARA` gave `{"charge": "170.00"}` for `Pickup/Collect` and for `Send`, and `"120.00"` for `D2B` and for `B2B`; `TINKUNE` to `TINKUNE` gave `"99.00"` for `Pickup/Collect`. Failure modes: an invalid type (`B2D`) answers 200 `{"charge": "0"}`, so a zero charge is a failed quote; an unknown branch name answers HTTP 500 `text/plain`, which **contradicts** the 200 HTML page of the 2026-11-19 note above, so any reply that is not 2xx JSON is a failed quote. The rate's shape is no longer a reason to wait before building the launch's checkout (launch part 4, [12 §9](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#9-single-seller-launch-r1-s)); production rates, the production branch list and NCM's confirmation are still needed before that checkout goes live, and mapping a buyer's district to a destination branch name is a design point, not a VX. | The `CourierProvider` port of [ADR-0019](adr/0019-courier-integration-in-r1.md) and the tables M6 adds for booking and tracking (booking attempts, a courier event inbox, branch codes). ### 3.2 Question packs by verifier

Paid advice is the scarcest resource, so questions are batched into one pack per verifier. Each pack asks for a written answer per item that cites the section of the **Nepali** original.

- **Legal counsel (one engagement before M2, one follow-up before launch):**
  - VX-01, VX-02, VX-03, VX-04, VX-09, and the legal part of VX-08.
  - Legal parts of OD-02, OD-07 (with OD-35, vendor-paid COD refunds), OD-16, OD-17, OD-21, OD-24 and OD-26.
  - Advertisement (Regulation) Act 2076 under VX-03: consent wording and records for marketing email and SMS, advertiser identification (s9), and whether push, Viber or WhatsApp messages count.
  - Under VX-02: does a one-way salted hash (scrypt) meet Directive 2082 s8(1) "encrypted form" for passwords (01 REG-14)? When does the s9(3) 7-day refund deadline start? Under VX-04: does the CPA s14(5) 15-day sealed-goods window apply (OD-06)?
  - Plus: does Directive 2082 s8(2) allow a halt limited to the affected function (R-10)? Does ETA s43 intermediary protection reach a marketplace (R-04)? Must each vendor list on the DoCSCP portal (VX-02)?
- **Accountant (one engagement before M2, statement sign-off before launch):**
  - VX-05 (including reverse-charge VAT on foreign-currency invoices, R-32) and the tax part of VX-08, including whether the 7-year approximation is acceptable.
  - OD-04 (VAT on commission), OD-05 (commission invoice and TDS), OD-11, OD-26, OD-27 and OD-36 (tax on a failed or returned payout).
  - Sign-off of the vendor statement format produced from `ledger_entries`.
- **Provider onboarding (start at M7, see R-31):**
  - VX-06 and VX-07 confirmations in writing: production hosts, the redirect parameter name, session expiry, refund method, auth and amount unit, handling of duplicate merchant IDs, whether server-to-server notification is available on request, and whether the merchant agreement allows selling third-party vendors' goods.
  - For VX-14: SMS sender-ID and IP allow-list rules.
- **Provider onboarding, Nepal Can Move (when DripNepal's merchant account is opened, before M6 code starts):**
  - VX-16, VX-17 and VX-18: the API documentation and sandbox, pickups from many vendors on one account, and how COD is remitted and reported (R-33).
  - For the single-seller launch (R1-S), before its checkout goes live: the rate call and the branch list on production, the rate `type` that matches a door-to-door booking, and how NCM spells district names in its branch list (VX-16).
- **Product owner with the company's bank (before the staging server, M5 at the latest):**
  - VX-15 card and FX part: the route and limits for paying USD or EUR invoices from Nepal (R-32). The staging server is the first paid plan ([11 §1.8](11-deployment-and-operations.md#18-paying-for-it-from-nepal-r-32-vx-15)).
- **Tech lead (in-house):**
  - VX-10: the local levels and names the MoFAGA cross-check of 2026-11-22 left open (04 §21.4), and the boundary-change process.
  - VX-11 re-check before M9.
  - VX-12 formatter, with the product owner.
  - VX-14 seeded-inbox test.
  - VX-15 latency benchmark from at least three Nepali ISPs (for example via friends' home connections or RIPE Atlas probes) and control-panel prices.

| Rank | ID   | Risk                                         | Score | Next action                                                 |

| 1    | R-04 | Counterfeit goods                            | 16    | Decide OD-20 and OD-21 before M3                            |
| 1    | R-07 | Small-team bus factor                        | 16    | Every runbook executed once by its non-author before launch |
| 1    | R-12 | Scope creep vs 1–2 developers                | 16    | Hold the R1 list; each addition names what moves out        |
| 4    | R-01 | Platform-collects model needs an NRB licence | 15    | Legal pack before any merchant application (VX-01)          |
| 4    | R-24 | Cross-tenant data exposure                   | 15    | RF-01 guard in M0; T-SEC-001 on every seller endpoint       |

### 4.2 Register

L = likelihood, I = impact, S = score (§1.7). Every risk's status is Open as of 2026-09-26 (R-32 added in the review of 2026-09-26). The owner reports at the weekly review when a trigger fires.

| ID   | Risk (cause → consequence)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | L   | I   | S   | Preventive mitigations                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Detective controls; trigger or early warning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Owner                                               | Links                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --- | --- | --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-01 | **Legality of the platform-collects model.** Counsel or NRB treats collecting gateway payments for vendors into DripNepal's merchant account as unlicensed PSP or aggregator activity (PSA 2075 s5) → R1.1 blocked, or enforcement.                                                                                                                                                                | 3   | 5   | 15  | R1 is COD-only with no platform custody [Confirmed]. ## 5. Decisions that block implementation, by milestone

This matrix answers "what must be decided before we can start or finish this milestone?". **Blocks code** means developers would build the wrong thing without the decision. **Blocks exit** means code can proceed on the safe default of the last column, but the milestone (or launch) cannot close. An OD or VX not listed for a milestone does not block it.

This matrix is authoritative for decision gates. [12 §3](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#3-releases-and-milestones-at-a-glance) and each milestone of 12, [00 §7.3](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#73-what-blocks-which-milestone) and [01 §11.1](01-product-requirements.md#111-external-dependencies) follow it. Several ODs gate more than one milestone, for the reasons given in their §2.2 rows: OD-04 (rate at M2 exit, basis at M5 start), OD-05 (M2 exit for the seller-agreement text, M7 code for the remittance flow, and launch), OD-11 (M2 exit for the seller-agreement text, and launch), OD-06 (values at M6 exit, hold revision at M8 exit), OD-22 (M2 if kit dashboard blocks are wanted, M4 if storefront kit blocks are), OD-26 (M6 exit and launch), OD-27 (its COD question at the R1 launch gate, the payout computation at M8 exit), OD-49 (M2 code for the verification reveal, M7 code for the refund-recipient reveal, M8 code for the payout reveal), and OD-56 and OD-57 (M0 baseline tables, M6 operations); see Consistency notes 1, 8, 20 and 25. The R1-S row lists the gates of the single-seller launch (2026-11-23), which is built after M1 part 6b from parts of M2 to M7 (the M1 exit items follow as 12 §9 says); the milestone rows keep their gates for the full R1 (note 53).

**Reading the matrix for the next four weeks.** The M0 code blockers OD-01, OD-12, OD-13 and OD-25 and the M0 exit item OD-23 were decided on 2026-09-30, the code blockers OD-56 and OD-57 on 2026-11-05, and the exit item OD-55 on 2026-11-07 (§2.3), so no open decision blocks M0 code, and OD-59 (the demo seeder) was decided on 2026-11-21 as its safe default, so no open decision blocks the M0 exit either; the OD-25 spike still runs in the first days, and its outcome is recorded when it ends. OD-24, OD-50 and OD-60 were decided on 2026-11-22, so no open decision blocks M1 code; counsel's VX-03 answer can still reopen OD-24 before the M1 exit. The first engagements with counsel and the accountant (§3.2) must be booked during M0–M1: their answers feed the M2 seller agreement, which is the first point where legal and tax questions block real vendors. Since 2026-11-23 the next work is the single-seller launch (R1-S): OD-15 must be decided before its part 2 and VX-12 before its part 3, and OD-63, with OD-11 and OD-26, should go to counsel and the accountant now, because go-live waits for them.

## Consistency notes for editor

1. **OD-04 timing.** The commission snapshot is written at `placeOrder` step 9 ([05 §4.3](05-order-payment-and-inventory-lifecycles.md#43-step-by-step)), which is M5, and the rate goes into the seller agreement. This register therefore requires the **basis** before the first M5 `placeOrder` PR and the **rate** before the seller agreement is final (M2 exit). Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: [12 §3](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#3-releases-and-milestones-at-a-glance) and each milestone follow §5 (12 note 9); 05 owns no milestones, so it needs no change.
2. **OD-27 scope.** [05 §7.9](05-order-payment-and-inventory-lifecycles.md#79-tax_withholding-reserved-pending-od-27) and 01 mark OD-27 as blocking M8 payouts. Income Tax Act s95A(8) deems the 1% collected even when it was not, with joint liability (s95A(11)). COD sales in R1 have no payout, so whether the tax applies to them must be answered before R1 launch. §5 lists this as an R1 launch-gate item. Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 00 §7.2 and 12 §3 (M7 exit, "OD-27 (COD)") follow.
3. **Refund method vocabulary.** This document uses the three values of `refunds_method_check` ([04a §12.4](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#124-refunds)): `gateway_api`, `gateway_manual` and `manual_transfer`. Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 04a, 05 §6.7 and the openapi.yaml `RefundMethod` enum use the same three values.
4. **"Rs" or "Rs." prefix (open, owner 08).** "Rs 12,34,567.50" without a period: 00 A-11, 02 AC-J00-05, 03 §6, 04 §18.1, [ADR-0007](adr/0007-money-integer-minor-units.md) and the [08 §5.11](08-ui-ux-and-design-system.md#511-money-and-numeral-display-vx-12) recommendation. "Rs." with a period: suggested by the regulatory research for English text and invoices ([research: nepal-regulatory-locale](research/nepal-regulatory-locale.md)). This register's M4 safe default follows A-11 ("Rs"). [08](08-ui-ux-and-design-system.md) decides under VX-12, and the other docs change to match. Updated in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: no planning document uses "Rs." for the UI (08 notes 1 and 48); the prefix stays [Verify-external VX-12].
5. **OD-06 default.** The default `ledger_hold_days = 7` ([04a §15.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#151-platform_settings)) equals the return window. This register recommends raising the hold to at least the return window + 7 days before M8 payouts. Doc 04 may want a note next to the default. If OD-06 adopts the CPA s14(5) 15-day sealed-goods window, 04a §15.1 needs a second key and a product flag, and 05 §6.6 a precondition.
6. **OD-16 vs `shops.business_type`.** [04a §6.1](04a-data-dictionary-tables.md#61-shops) allows `business_type` `individual` or `registered_business` (`shops_business_type_check`). E-Commerce Act s16(b) asks sellers for evidence of business registration, which may rule out unregistered individuals. The `individual` value should stay, labelled [Open] OD-16, until counsel answers.
7. **Current route prefix.** The repository's dashboard prefix is `/shop/:shopSlug` (start/routes/shops.ts:27), although the title of commit 0282605 says `/shops/:shopSlug/dashboard`. 00 and this document describe the current state as `/shop/:shopSlug`, which matches the code. Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: OD-12 (Decided 2026-09-30) moves these routes to `/seller/{shopSlug}` in M0, so nothing is left to align.
8. **Milestone gates set by this register (register is authoritative; target [12](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md)).** OD-04: M2 exit (rate) and M5 code (basis). OD-05 and OD-11: M2 exit (seller-agreement text) besides launch. OD-06: M6 exit (values) and M8 exit (hold revision). OD-22: M2 (only if kit dashboard blocks are wanted) and M4. OD-26: M6 exit and launch. OD-27: the R1 launch gate for the COD question besides M8. 00 §7.3 and 01 §11.1 follow §5. Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 12 §3 and each milestone follow §5 (12 note 9), and 00 note 6 is closed (00 §7.3 now also lists VX-03 and VX-04 at M6, VX-04 at M7, "with VX-05" and "OD-14 revisited if staff grew" at M8, R-23 at M9 and VX-10 at M2).
9. **VX-03 widened to the Advertisement (Regulation) Act 2076 (resolved here).** 01 REG-26 and FR-IAM-013 and 04a §5.1 and §14.3 cite VX-03 for marketing consent, so VX-03 was widened instead of adding a new VX. 00 §9.5 and 04a `users.marketing_email_consent_at` labelled the Act [Verified-doc] with no VX. Updated in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 04a now adds "obligations [Verify-external VX-03]"; the 00 §9.5 source row may still add VX-03 (optional).
10. **Foreign-currency invoices (resolved here).** VX-15 now asks the card and FX question explicitly, VX-05 asks about reverse-charge VAT, and R-32 is the risk row. Updated in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: the bank question is due before the staging server (M5 at the latest), as [11 §1.8](11-deployment-and-operations.md#18-paying-for-it-from-nepal-r-32-vx-15) and 12 note 7 say; §3.2, VX-15, R-32 and the §5 M5 exit follow, and the §5 M7 exit keeps VX-15 for prices, limits and latency. Done in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 00 §7.3 adds the VX-15 FX route to the M5 exit and keeps VX-15 at M7 for prices, limits and latency (the FX route by M5, R-32), the 00 §8 VX-15 row blocks the M5 exit and M7, and 12 §3 and §5.5 list the FX route as an M5 exit item.
11. **Assumptions from 05 not yet in 00's A-register (target [00 §6](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#6-proposed-defaults-and-assumptions-register)).** Recorded here as [Assumption] against the rows they affect: the 24 h needs-review stock hold (R-11); the delivery reattempt limit of 3 (R-02); the platform paying COD refunds and recovering them from the vendor (OD-07, R-03); the ledger availability group rule, decided per delivery-posting group by its net (OD-06); second approval for ledger adjustments above Rs 10,000 (OD-14); the 10 s provider timeout (R-11). None is a separate decision, so no new OD was opened; 00 §6 should add A-xx entries for them. Updated in the consistency review, 2026-09-30: 00 now registers A-36 (the platform bears gateway (MDR) fees from R1.1, and they never enter the vendor ledger, 05 §7.1); the seller-agreement text states it at the M2 exit beside OD-04 and OD-05 (§5), and no OD was opened. The six assumptions above are still not in 00 §6 (00 note 9). 51. **OD-62 and the signed-in resend (2026-11-23, M1 part 2a review).** §2.2 registers OD-62, found in the Fable review of M1 part 2a: verifying an email does not prove that whoever set the password holds the mailbox, so a stranger's sign-up with someone else's address can become an `active` account when the holder clicks the link. It blocks the M1 exit, not code, with the sign-up as built as its safe default; §5's M1 row, [12 §3 and §5.1](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#51-m1-identity--accounts-r1), [00 §7.2 and §7.3](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#72-remaining-open-decisions) and [07 TM-09](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#tm-09-account-recovery-abuse-and-enumeration) list it. §2.3 logs the tech lead's ruling, not an OD, that a new verification link is for signed-in users only, which closes [02 note 27](02-user-journeys-and-acceptance-criteria.md#8-consistency-notes-for-editor).
52. **Interim home page (2026-11-23, M1 part 3).** §2.3 logs, as a "Not an OD" entry, the tech lead's ruling that `/` gets an interim home page in the `storefront/home` component until M4, because every redirect after sign-in and after a verified link lands there and found no route in the browser walk-through ([08 note 80](08-ui-ux-and-design-system.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)).
53. **Single-seller launch (2026-11-23).** §2.3 logs three product-owner decisions as "Not an OD" entries: the single-seller launch R1-S with its scope and deferrals, guest checkout (revising Q6), and Nepal Can Move's live rate as the delivery fee with the [Assumption A-61] fallback. §2.2 registers OD-63, the legal posture of the owner selling their own goods, which conflicts with the 01 §5.4 non-goal "DripNepal-owned stock" and REG-01; it blocks R1-S go-live, not the build, and is not decided here. §2.1 and the introduction follow. VX-16 records the rate call observed on NCM's demo host, read-only, on 2026-11-23, including an unknown branch answering 500 `text/plain` where the 2026-11-19 note had a 200 HTML page (marked as a contradiction), and its Blocks cell adds R1-S go-live; the NCM question pack adds the production checks. §5 adds the R1-S row (code blockers by part, go-live items, what does not apply to a single seller, what waits) and its reading paragraph. R-02, R-12 and R-21 follow the decisions. Round 2, after the Fable review: §2.2 and §2.3 record OD-44 as decided by the product owner, (b) with the `single_operator_mode` exception, and §5 drops it from the M7 and R1-S rows; the VX-16 note uses plain words under [Verify-external VX-16] instead of a label that 00 §1.2 does not define; the R1-S row and its paragraph say the launch follows M1 part 6b; the first 2026-11-23 entry uses "their own shop". Round 3: the OD-44 row and a dated edit of its §2.3 entry say the exception runs under `single_operator_mode`, set `true` at go-live, and that the phone confirmation's route is left to part 6; §5's R1-S row keeps OD-14 under "wait for the full R1" with the reason.
54. **OD-64 registered (2026-11-23, R1-S part 1b).** §2.2 registers OD-64, found while building the shop profile edit: R1-S part 1b lets the owner change the business type and identifiers of an `active` shop directly ([06 note 64](06-api-design.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)), because 12 §9 enters them in the profile and the owner's shop is the only shop, while the multi-vendor flow has staff review them at application (07 TM-31). It blocks M2 code, where `updateShopProfile` and `adminUpdateShop` take the rule, and not R1-S, whose build is its safe default; §5's M2 row lists it, and [00 §7.2 and §7.3](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#72-remaining-open-decisions) and [12 §3 and §5.2](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#52-m2-shop-onboarding--membership-r1) follow.
55. **OD-44 built (2026-11-24, R1-S part 1c-ii).** The OD-44 row of §2.2 notes that the check is built on the seven admin operations that act on a shop or its orders, as [07 §4.1](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#41-principles) item 4 and §4.5 record (07 note 58). The decision itself is unchanged; `single_operator_mode` stays `false` until go-live.
56. **OD-15 for R1-S (2026-11-24).** The product owner agreed ("go ahead") to build R1-S part 2 on the safe defaults, with the values kept configurable. The safe defaults are OD-15's recommendation, as the tech lead reads it. The reference seeders already hold it ([04 §21](04-domain-model-and-data-dictionary.md)): audience men, women and kids, multi-valued; `apparel_size`, `shoe_size_eu` and `waist_size_in`; platform colours with swatches. So §5's R1-S row lists no code blocker for part 2. The recommendation's own condition, a check against the launch catalogue before seeding, becomes a go-live item, because the owner's catalogue is the launch catalogue; that is the tech lead's reading too. OD-15 is not decided and stays open for the full R1 (M3), where other vendors' catalogues count. The OD-15 row of §2.2 points here.
57. **OD-65 registered (2026-11-24, R1-S part 2d-i).** §2.2 registers OD-65, found while building the uploads: the development and CI S3 server, since MinIO no longer publishes container images and CI's service needs a workflow change. It blocks no code; its safe default is the in-memory fake and the canned-HTTP adapter tests ([10 note 89](10-testing-and-quality-gates.md#consistency-notes-for-editor)), with the staging smoke as the latest real check. §5's R1-S row lists it under part 2, and [00 §7.2](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#72-remaining-open-decisions), [03 note 40](03-system-architecture.md#consistency-notes-for-editor), [11 note 124](11-deployment-and-operations.md#consistency-notes-for-editor) and [12 note 76](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#consistency-notes-for-editor) follow.
58. **OD-65 decided (2026-11-25).** §2.2 marks OD-65 decided and §2.3 logs it: SeaweedFS for development and CI, after a probe showed it holds a presigned PUT to its signed `Content-Type` and refuses a stale `If-Match`, as R2 does. §5's R1-S row follows. [00 §7.2](00-context-assumptions-and-questions.md#72-remaining-open-decisions), [03 §3.5](03-system-architecture.md#35-object-storage-layout), [07 §7.3](07-security-threat-model-and-permissions.md#73-csp-and-security-headers), [09 §6.1](09-code-structure-and-engineering-standards.md#61-variable-catalogue), [10 §3.6](10-testing-and-quality-gates.md#36-provider-fakes-email-storage-and-jobs), [11 §2.2](11-deployment-and-operations.md#22-development), [12 §9](12-roadmap-and-backlog.md#9-single-seller-launch-r1-s), ADR-0013 and ADR-0016 follow.
59. **The `supabase-postgres-best-practices` skill (2026-11-26).** §2.3 logs the product owner's decision to track Supabase's PostgreSQL skill, after the licence check and read-through that [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) requires. It changes no OD, VX or risk: where the skill disagrees with the documents (money, tenant isolation, primary keys, pagination, migrations, enum types, queues, connection settings, roles and server settings, search), the documents win, and its `NOTICE.md` lists the differences.
60. **OD-23 re-decided (2026-11-26).** The product owner replaced the MIT ruling of 2026-09-30 with option (b): the code is proprietary, all rights reserved, and the repository stays public for GitHub's free CI, branch protection and secret scanning. §2.2 and §2.3 record it; the VX-13 question, R-15 and §5's M0 row no longer call the repository MIT, and ADR-0015 has a dated note: its revisit trigger fired, but OD-22 (a) and its decisions are unchanged, because the source is still public. The documents that restate OD-23 follow in the same pull request.
